{
  "candidates": ["F32.0", "F33.0", "F43.22"],
  "note": "The patient endorsed both sleep disturbances and depressed mood with anhedonia, which supports the inclusion of major depressive disorder (F32.0, F33.0) and adjustment disorder with depressed mood (F43.22). Insomnia disorder (G47.01) is less likely as the sleep issues are better explained by mood symptoms. Non-restorative sleep and early morning awakening are common in depression. Adjustment disorder remains possible if the symptoms are in response to a specific stressor and not severe enough for major depression. No features of insomnia disorder alone are present.",
  "is_final": false

Like, I’ll be sitting in my condo—the place I worked so hard to get—and instead of feeling proud or comfortable, I just feel like I want to cry.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed that you’ve lost interest or pleasure in most activities, even ones you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I have. I used to go to salsa class, and I just stopped going. And I have my paints sitting out on the table, but I just look at them and feel nothing… like they belong to somebody else.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down or depressed most of the day, nearly every day, rather than just at certain times or in certain situations?\n\nPatient: Yeah… it’s been most of the day, most days lately. I wake up already feeling heavy and empty, and it kind of stays with me. Even being in my own condo, which I worked so hard for, I just feel like crying instead of feeling good about it.\n\nDoctor: Has this depressed mood been going on for at least 2 years, or is it something that has developed more recently?\n\nPatient: It’s more recent than that. I’d say this depressed, guilty fog has been around for about five months now… but it got a lot worse after I bought the condo.\n\nDoctor: Before these five months of feeling depressed, were you feeling down or having low periods most days for a lot of the time, even before buying the condo?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean, I’ve had bad days before, but I don’t think it was most days for a long time like this. This feels more like something that really set in recently.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time before this recent five months where you felt down or lost interest in things for most of the day, nearly every day, for at least two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t really remember a time before this where it was most of the day, nearly every day for two weeks. I’ve had rough patches, but nothing that clearly stands out like this.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time where you felt unusually high, elated, or extremely energetic, where you were doing things more than usual or felt like you didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Yeah… earlier this month I had a stretch where I felt really energized, almost lit up, like I could handle anything. But I was also more short-tempered. I snapped at my mom and even my supervisor over small questions that normally wouldn’t bother me.\n\nDoctor: How long did you feel that unusually energized and 'lit up' mood, where you snapped at people and felt like you could handle anything?\n\nPatient: It was part of this up-and-down thing that’s been really noticeable for about a month now, since the mortgage became real. The charged-up feeling would come in stretches, and then I’d crash after.\n\nDoctor: How many days in a row did you feel that unusually energized and 'lit up' mood, where you felt like you could handle anything and didn’t need much sleep?\n\nPatient: I can’t pin it down to an exact number of days in a row. The really noticeable back-and-forth between feeling charged up like that and then crashing has been going on for about a month, since the mortgage stuff became real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of a time when you felt that unusually energized, 'lit up' mood for four days or more without it being interrupted by feelings of depression or crashing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can say it was four full days straight without any crash. What I can say is the really obvious back-and-forth between that charged-up feeling and crashing has been going on about a month, since the mortgage became real.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time when you felt that unusually energized, 'lit up' mood for at least four days straight, even if it later switched to feeling down or crashing afterward?\n\nPatient: I’m still not totally sure about four days straight. What I know is the really clear cycling — that charged-up stretch and then the crash — has been going on for about a month, since the mortgage became real.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.       "Onset of manic episode prior to current depressive episode confirmed",
      "No better explanation by another psychotic or mood disorder"
    ]
  }
}
